Self-check: Part 2 Review

Items 4 and 12 show real tsc output (npx tsc -b on a copy of the HQ at the end of Lesson 6.3; the messages are the ones Lessons 4.3 and 6.2 print).

Q1
In development, the Network tab shows each page's request twice, the first one canceled. In the preview build there is one. Why?
A) The API answers slowly the first time
B) A missing dependency array
C) Strict Mode runs each effect, its cleanup and the effect again in development; the cleanup aborts the first request
D) The browser retries every failed request
Why: Strict Mode's start, clean up, start is development only: it proves the cleanup works. The aborted request is the cleanup doing its job (Lesson 4.1).
A is wrong: Speed has nothing to do with it: the count is always two.
B is wrong: No array would send hundreds of requests, not two.
D is wrong: The first request was canceled by our own cleanup, not retried.
Q2
Without an annotation, what is the type of error in .catch((error) => ...), and what does the HQ write instead?
A) Error; nothing to change
B) any; the HQ writes (error: unknown) and narrows with instanceof ApiError
C) ApiError; the HQ casts it
D) never; catch never runs
Why: A promise can reject with anything, and .catch's parameter is any, so error.no.such.thing compiles. unknown forces a check first: describeError(error) narrows with instanceof.
A is wrong: Anything can be thrown, not only Error objects.
C is wrong: TypeScript cannot know the rejection's type.
D is wrong: Catch runs whenever the promise rejects.
Q3
A custom Hook returns early (if (!query) return null) before it calls useEffect. Which check reports it?
A) npx tsc -b, with an error on the return
B) npm run lint: oxlint's rules-of-hooks error, "called conditionally"
C) React, as a TypeScript error in the browser
D) Nothing: Hooks may be called conditionally inside custom Hooks
Why: Hooks must be called in the same order on every render, in custom Hooks too. tsc is silent; oxlint's react-hooks(rules-of-hooks) reports an error (Lesson 4.2).
A is wrong: Types are fine: tsc says nothing.
C is wrong: The browser never sees TypeScript.
D is wrong: Custom Hooks follow the same rules as components.
Q4
A timer Hook keeps its interval id in a ref, and tsc prints this. Which declaration does Lesson 4.3 use?
import { useRef } from 'react'

export function useSweepTimer() {
  const timerRef = useRef<number>()
  return timerRef
}
A) useRef<number | null>(null)
B) useState<number>()
C) useRef<number>()!
D) useRef<any>()
Why: React 19's useRef needs a start value. Nothing is running yet, so the honest start is null, and the type says so: number | null. A ref, not state: the id is never shown, so changing it should not render.
B is wrong: State would render again on every start and stop for a value nobody sees.
C is wrong: Still no argument: the same TS2554.
D is wrong: Still no argument, and any turns checking off.
Q5
On the event page, why does the back link sit OUTSIDE the ErrorBoundary and <Suspense> that wrap the details?
A) Links cannot be inside a boundary
B) So it renders faster
C) Error boundaries remove everything in the app
D) Whatever is inside a boundary is replaced by its fallback: outside, the link stays usable while the details load or fail
Why: The nearest boundary replaces its whole subtree with the fallback. Put boundaries around the part that can wait or fail, and keep the way out outside them (Lesson 4.4).
A is wrong: Anything can be inside.
B is wrong: Placement is about what stays on screen.
C is wrong: Only without any boundary does React remove the whole app.
Q6
Why must a reducer return a new object instead of calling state.marks.set(...) and returning state?
React compares the old and new state with Object.is: the same object means "nothing changed", so the screen does not update (and Strict Mode's second call would apply the change twice). A copy with the change keeps the old state intact; ReadonlyMap makes tsc refuse set
Why: Lesson 5.1's rules: pure, no in-place change, no side effects, the same state back only when nothing changed.
Q7
Which value is the best candidate for context in the HQ?
A) The cleanup that one EventCard shows
B) The text in the events search box
C) The signed-in session, read by the header, the event cards and the data Hooks at many depths
D) The open/closed state of one dialog
Why: Context is for values many components at different depths need. A value one component or one parent passes down is clearer as a prop or local state (Lesson 5.2's context vs props table).
A is wrong: A plain prop from the list.
B is wrong: Page state (and, after 6.2, the address).
D is wrong: Local state of the component that owns the dialog.
Q8
Without the compiler, a memo component still renders every time its parent does. Its parent passes onRemove={(id) => removeSupply(id)}. Why?
A) memo only works on class components
B) The parent's state is too large
C) memo compares props with Object.is, and the arrow is a new function on every render
D) Strict Mode disables memo
Why: memo and new function props cancel out: keep the function the same with useCallback, or let the React Compiler (on in the HQ) do it (Lesson 5.3).
A is wrong: memo is for function components.
B is wrong: Size does not matter; identity does.
D is wrong: Strict Mode doubles renders in development; it does not switch memo off.
Q9
The caller writes <Tabs>, <TabList>, <Tab id=...> and <TabPanel id=...> and arranges them freely. Which pattern is that, and how do the parts share the selected tab?
A) Render props, through a function child
B) A polymorphic component, through its as prop
C) A configuration array, through one big prop
D) Compound components, through a private context that Tabs provides
Why: Compound components share state through a private context with a throwing Hook (the 5.2 pattern), so the caller keeps control of the markup (Lesson 5.4).
A is wrong: No function is passed.
B is wrong: as changes which element renders.
C is wrong: That is the alternative Lesson 5.4 compares it with.
Q10
The event page shows the cleanup from :eventId. Why does it put key={event.id} on the tabs?
Moving from one cleanup to another stays on the same route, so React keeps the same components and their state (the selected tab). A new key per cleanup makes React start fresh
Why: Measured in Lesson 6.1: without the key, the old selected tab stayed and no panel showed.
Q11
With a loader, opening /sites and coming Back restores the scroll position; the events list, still on useApi, comes back at the top. Why the difference?
A loader loads the data BEFORE the page renders, so the page appears complete and the router can restore the scroll. A page that fetches after it renders appears empty first ("Loading…"), too short to scroll to where you were
Why: Render then fetch vs load then render (Lesson 6.3). Lesson 7.1's cache fixes the events list.
Q12
tsc prints this for one component, while the same typo with an untyped useParams() compiles. What did the type argument buy?
import { useParams } from 'react-router'

export function VolunteerHeading() {
  const { volunterId } = useParams<'volunteerId'>()
  return <h2>Volunteer {volunterId}</h2>
}
A) Nothing: the parameter is still string | undefined and nothing else changes
B) 'volunteerId' names the route's parameter, so a misspelled name is an error instead of a silent undefined
C) It checks that the route exists
D) It makes the value a string
Why: useParams<'volunteerId'>() gives an object with exactly that key; its value is still string | undefined, so narrow it. Nothing connects the name to the route list in data mode (Lesson 6.2).
A is wrong: It turned a silent bug into an error.
C is wrong: In data mode nothing checks the route list.
D is wrong: The value may still be undefined.

Exit Ticket

  1. Any idea with a place in their code. Weak answers name an idea without a file: ask "where?" at the hand-in.
  2. Their own; it should match the check-in's next step. If it does not, the check-in did not land: follow up in writing.
  3. Their own; collect them for the course feedback.

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • A2 B (return the promise). Has not read the message's second line: an effect returns nothing or a cleanup. Lesson 4.1's async-effect card again.
  • A4 D (as HTMLInputElement). Reaches for assertions: the same habit as as StatusFilter in A12. Ask: when is the ref null?
  • A7 A (tsc reports it). Believes the switch is checked without assertNever: A16 tests the fixed version.
  • A14 A (redirect to sign-in). Mixes up 401 and 403: guests sign in, signed-in people without the role get 403.
  • Self-check 3 A (tsc). Thinks the type checker enforces the Rules of Hooks: it is the linter's job.
React + TypeScript: Build a Modern Web App · Answer Key

Part 3: Production React

Sessions 18 to 26. Modules 7 to 11: server state with TanStack Query and mutations, client state with Zustand, forms at scale with React Hook Form and Zod, testing with Vitest, Testing Library, MSW and Playwright, performance and the build, a free deployment with CI, then the final project and next steps. Opens with a Parts 1 and 2 refresher and catch-up, and ends with the final project showcase and final assessment.

Instructor Kit · Answer Key

Session 18: TanStack Query

Answers and reasoning for every Session 18 item: the refresher, the self-check, the predict-then-check items, the catch-up check and the lab reference outputs, the lesson page's own quiz and the exit ticket.

Session 18 12 self-check items Confidential

Start Here · Refresher

  1. **R1.** Lesson 4.1's LoadState had three kinds. Name them, and say what TypeScript lets you read in each. **Answer:** loading (nothing), error (the message or error), ready (the data). A switch or an if on kind narrows the union; TanStack Query's result is the same idea with status as its tag.
  2. **R2.** useApi(key, load) from Lesson 4.2: what was the key for, and what happened when it changed? **Answer:** It said what the answer depends on; a new key started a new request (and kept the old data while it loaded). Today's query keys are that idea, shared across components.
  3. **R3.** A guest opens /volunteers. What runs first, and what do they see? **Answer:** The organizersOnly guard loader (Lesson 6.3) throws a redirect to /sign-in?next=%2Fvolunteers before any request; after signing in they land back on the crew list.
  4. **R4.** Why does the events page have no loader, when the sites page does? **Answer:** Its search box changes the address on every letter; a loader on that route breaks typing (Lesson 6.3 measured the caret jumping). The cache gives it the loader's benefits today.

7.1 · TanStack Query: self-check

Q1
Which of these is server state in Shoreline Crew HQ?
A) Whether the Cancel cleanup dialog is open
B) The text typed into the events search box
C) The check-in desk's undo history
D) The roster of today's cleanup
Why: shoreline-api owns the roster; the HQ only holds a copy that can change behind its back (another organizer, another phone). That is what TanStack Query's cache is for (Lesson 7.1, the server state table).
A is wrong: Only that component cares, and nothing outside the browser knows about it: component state.
B is wrong: The search lives in the address since Lesson 6.2: client state that a link can reproduce.
C is wrong: Only this desk knows what it just did: client state in a reducer.
Q2
An organizer filters the events list. Which key does the events list query need?
A) ['events']
B) ['events', 'list', search, viewer]
C) ['events', 'list', viewer]
D) ['events', viewer, 'list']
Why: Everything the answer depends on, general to specific: the search changes the cleanups, and organizers also get drafts. Starting with 'events' lets one invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['events'] }) reach every list and detail in Lesson 7.2.
A is wrong: Every list, detail and roster would share one entry: the second search shows the first one's cleanups.
C is wrong: Leaves out the search: typing "pelican" changes the address and sends no request (measured in the lesson).
D is wrong: Works, but puts the viewer before the kind of data: keys go general to specific, the viewer last.
Q3
tsc prints this for the lesson's Scratch.tsx. What is list.data before any check, and what removes the undefined?
src/Scratch.tsx:12:8 - error TS2345: Argument of type 'Page<CleanupEvent> | undefined' is not assignable to parameter of type 'number'.
src/Scratch.tsx:13:8 - error TS2345: Argument of type 'CleanupEventDetail' is not assignable to parameter of type 'number'.
src/Scratch.tsx:15:10 - error TS2345: Argument of type 'Page<CleanupEvent>' is not assignable to parameter of type 'number'.
Found 3 errors in the same file, starting at: src/Scratch.tsx:12
Page<CleanupEvent> | undefined; checking list.isSuccess (or an early return on it) narrows it to Page<CleanupEvent>
Why: A query result is a union tagged by status, like Lesson 4.1's LoadState: data exists only on 'success'. The second error shows useSuspenseQuery's data has no undefined at all: it never returns without data. No type argument anywhere: the types come from the factory's query function.
Q4
The API runs with --latency=800. On /events you type "pelican". The events query uses placeholderData: keepPreviousData. What does the page show while the new list loads?
A) "Loading the cleanups…" until the 3 matches arrive
B) An empty list, then the 3 matches
C) The 15 cleanups, until the 3 matches replace them
D) The 3 matches at once, from the cache
Why: A new search is a new key with no data yet; keepPreviousData keeps the last answer on screen meanwhile (isPlaceholderData is true). Measured in the lesson: "Loading the cleanups…" never showed (Exercise 1 adds "Updating…").
A is wrong: That is what happens without placeholderData: the query goes back to 'pending'.
B is wrong: Nothing empties the list: the old data stays until the new data replaces it.
D is wrong: "pelican" was never asked before: there is no entry under that key yet.
Q5
The QueryClient's staleTime is 0 (the default). Maya opens Weekend Cove Cleanup, then presses Back to the events list. What happens?
A) The list appears at once from the cache, and its requests run again behind it
B) The list shows "Loading the cleanups…" until the requests come back
C) Nothing is sent: the list is in the cache
D) The list is thrown away and fetched from scratch
Why: Every answer is stale at once with staleTime 0, so remounting the list refetches it, but a stale answer is still shown immediately. Measured in the lesson: Back sent /sites and /events?perPage=50 again, the list appearing at once. With the HQ's 30 seconds, nothing was sent.
B is wrong: A cached answer, fresh or stale, is shown at once; only a key with no entry waits.
C is wrong: That is the 30-second staleTime: fresh means no request.
D is wrong: Unused entries are kept for gcTime (5 minutes by default): stale is not gone.
Q6
With the HQ's retry rule below, which failure is tried again?
retry: (failureCount, error) =>
  !(error instanceof ApiError && error.status < 500) && failureCount < 2,
A) A 404 for /api/volunteers/v99, once more
B) A 401 after a session ended, twice more
C) A 403 for a volunteer opening the crew list
D) A 500 from the API, up to two more times
Why: Only failures that can change are retried: server errors and network failures, at most twice. A 404, 401 or 403 is an answer, not an accident: asking again gives the same answer (the API says No volunteer with that id. every time).
A is wrong: status < 500: not retried. The default retry made it wait 7.4 s and send 5 requests.
B is wrong: A 401 is below 500; the HQ signs out instead (Lesson 6.3).
C is wrong: Below 500: not retried.
Q7
Code from an older tutorial gives this. What is the version 5 name of this option, and what replaced the other two v4 options in the same object?
src/Scratch.tsx:9:5 - error TS2769: No overload matches this call.
  The last overload gave the following error.
    Object literal may only specify known properties, and 'cacheTime' does not exist in type 'UseQueryOptions<Site[], Error, Site[], string[]>'.

9     cacheTime: 10 * 60 * 1000,
      ~~~~~~~~~
gcTime; keepPreviousData: true became placeholderData: keepPreviousData, and onSuccess was removed from queries
Why: TypeScript stops at the first unknown property (TS2769 names only 'cacheTime'); fix it and the next one shows. isLoading changed meaning too (isPending is "no data yet"). The v5 renames table in Common Mistakes.
Q8
What does this loader line do?
await queryClient.query({ ...sitesQuery(), staleTime: 'static' })
A) Always fetches the sites, then caches them for good
B) Uses any cached copy of the sites, however old, and fetches only when there is none
C) Marks the sites as never stale, so the page never refreshes them
D) Prefetches the sites without waiting for them
Why: staleTime 'static' for this one call makes any cached copy good enough, so a page you have visited before opens at once. It is what the deprecated ensureQueryData did. The page then reads with useSuspenseQuery, whose own staleTime (30 s) still refreshes it in the background.
A is wrong: With a cached copy, nothing is fetched.
C is wrong: The 'static' applies to the loader's call only, not to the entry.
D is wrong: The loader awaits it: the page renders only once the sites are in the cache.
Q9
Why does EventPage build its key from the loader's {eventId, viewer} instead of reading useAuth()?
A) On sign-out the session changes before the loader re-runs; a key from the session would ask for the guest's copy of a draft too early
B) useAuth() cannot be called in a page
C) The loader's viewer is a different person
D) Query keys cannot hold values from context
Why: The loader turns a 404 into "Cleanup not found"; with the key from the loader, the page and its loader always agree, so a signed-out organizer on a draft sees that page instead of an error.
B is wrong: Pages call useAuth() all the time (the events page does).
C is wrong: It is the same person: read at a different moment.
D is wrong: Keys can hold any serializable value.
Q10
Someone removes viewer from the events list key. tsc says this. What would Maya see on the dev server (which does not type-check) after signing in?
src/api/queries.ts:44:45 - error TS6133: 'viewer' is declared but its value is never read.
Found 1 error in src/api/queries.ts:44
The guest's 15 cleanups under an empty Drafts heading: the cache served the guest's entry, because the key did not change when she signed in
Why: Who asks is part of the question. Here tsc caught it only because the parameter became unused (noUnusedParameters); leaving out search instead compiles cleanly (the kit ran it: no output). Make "everything the function uses goes in the key" a reflex.
Q11
The Impact tab's ErrorBoundary has no onRetry={reset}. The report failed to load; the person presses Try again. What happens?
A) A new request, and the report if the API is back
B) The same error at once, and no request
C) The whole page reloads
D) The query retries three times, then the error
Why: The failed query keeps its error in the cache; useSuspenseQuery sees it and throws it again before asking. reset from useQueryErrorResetBoundary() tells the queries below that the next render may try again (measured: 3 attempts, then Try again sent a 4th).
A is wrong: Only with the reset.
C is wrong: The boundary re-renders its children, nothing more.
D is wrong: Retries happened before the error reached the boundary; Try again without reset sends nothing.
Q12
Which value does a component read with useSuspenseQuery(eventQuery(eventId, viewer)).data?
A) CleanupEventDetail | undefined
B) Promise<CleanupEventDetail>
C) CleanupEventDetail
D) unknown, until you add a type argument
Why: useSuspenseQuery suspends while there is no data and throws errors to the nearest boundary, so when it returns, data is there: no undefined, no narrowing (the second TS2345 in Item 3's output).
A is wrong: That is useQuery's data before isSuccess.
B is wrong: use() from Lesson 4.4 took a promise; useSuspenseQuery returns the data.
D is wrong: The type comes from the factory's query function.

7.1 · TanStack Query: predict, then check

P1
Predict: the same address, asked by a guest and by Maya (organizer). Do the two answers have the same number of cleanups?
http://localhost:4000/api/events?perPage=50      (X-Total-Count?)
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
x-total-count: 15

HTTP/1.1 200 OK
x-total-count: 17
Why: Guests get the 15 public cleanups; organizers also get the two drafts. Same address, two answers: who asks is part of the question, so the viewer is part of the key (the kit asked a private shoreline-api, first without and then with Maya's token).
P2
Predict the status code and the message, then open it signed in as Maya. Should a query try it again?
http://localhost:4000/api/volunteers/v99
HTTP/1.1 404 Not Found
content-type: application/json; charset=utf-8

{
  "error": "not_found",
  "message": "No volunteer with that id."
}
Why: 404 with the API's message. Retrying cannot change it: the HQ's retry rule skips every ApiError below 500, so the error shows at once instead of after 7.4 seconds and 5 requests.
P3
Lesson 7.1, Exercise 2 (a): a fresh tab, the home page, then Sites, then Events. How many GET /api/sites?
One: the sites page's loader filled ['sites'], and the events page found it fresh.
Why: The lesson's measured answers (Exercise 2). For each, ask: is there an entry under that key, is it fresh, and does the retry rule apply to this error?
P4
Exercise 2 (b): on /events open Weekend Cove Cleanup, press Back, open it again within 30 seconds. How many GET /api/events/e08?
One, on the first visit; Back and the second visit used the fresh entries (staleTime 30 s).
Why: The lesson's measured answers (Exercise 2). For each, ask: is there an entry under that key, is it fresh, and does the retry rule apply to this error?
P5
Exercise 2 (c): signed in as Maya, open /volunteers/v99. How many requests for v99, and how long until the error (on npm run preview)?
One request and an immediate error on npm run preview (a 404 is not retried). The dev server showed 2: development mode does extra work, such as React's Strict Mode checks.
Why: The lesson's measured answers (Exercise 2). For each, ask: is there an entry under that key, is it fresh, and does the retry rule apply to this error?

What each task shows: (2) isSiteSummary proves every field before the value gets a type, and parseList<T>(data, isItem: (value: unknown) => value is T): T[] hands the predicate's type to the whole list: the shape of the HQ's getJSON(path, isT) and of today's query functions; (3) a switch on kind with assertNever in the default: add a fourth kind and tsc points at the default line (Argument of type … is not assignable to parameter of type 'never'). Query results are the same idea, tagged by status.

Lab 7.1: a cache with good keys (labs/module-18/l7_1-tanstack-query/starter → solution)

As shipped, npm test ends with:

Tests  8 failed (8)

The solution:

Tests  8 passed (8)

and npm run typecheck prints nothing after its two > lines. The solution's key and retry rule:

export function siteEventsQuery(siteId: string, viewer: string) {
  return queryOptions({
    queryKey: ['events', 'site', siteId, viewer],
    queryFn: ({ signal }) => getSiteEvents(siteId, signal),
  })
}

export function shouldRetry(failureCount: number, error: Error): boolean {
  return !(error instanceof ApiError && error.status < 500) && failureCount < 2
}
  • Task 1: the starter's key ['events', 'site', viewer] compiles and lints; with a 30-second staleTime the second site gets the first site's entry and no request. ['events'] still reaches the fixed key because keys match by prefix.
  • Task 2: after if (events.isPending) return … and if (events.isError) return …, events.data is CleanupEvent[] with no undefined; events.error.message is the API's message (No site with that id.). Watch for events.data?.map(...): it hides the narrowing instead of using it.
  • Task 3: the starter's failureCount < 3 retried the 404 three times (4 requests). The solution: 1 request for a 404, 3 for a 500 (the first try and two retries).
  • Stretch: placeholderData: keepPreviousData on the query and a line that says "Updating…" while events.isPlaceholderData.

The Lesson Page's Own Quick Quiz

Lesson 7.1 · TanStack Query
  1. C Page<CleanupEvent> | undefined: The type comes from getEvents through queryOptions, and undefined is there because a pending query has no data yet. Checking list.isSuccess narrows it to Page of CleanupEvent; useSuspenseQuery never has undefined.
  2. A One: The first visit fills the entry; ten seconds later it is still fresh (staleTime is 30 seconds), so the loader and the page both use it without asking the API. That was measured on the preview and the dev server.
  3. D The retry function in queries.ts skips errors below 500: The QueryClient's retry function returns false for an ApiError below 500, so a 404 fails on the first answer. With the default (three retries with growing pauses) the same page took over seven seconds.
  4. B The viewer is part of the key, so a guest's key points at a different entry: The key ends with the viewer: after sign-out it is guest, a different entry with no data yet, so the page asks the API for the guest's list (15 cleanups). The organizer's entry stays in memory until gcTime, but nothing reads it.
  5. C onRetry={reset}, with reset from useQueryErrorResetBoundary(): useSuspenseQuery rethrows a cached error unless the error reset boundary has been reset. The ErrorBoundary calls onRetry, which is reset from useQueryErrorResetBoundary, before rendering the children again, so the query fetches.

Exit Ticket

  1. A key for one site: ['events', 'site', siteId, viewer] (or any key that starts general and holds the site and the viewer).
  2. At once, and a request: the entry is cached but stale; stale answers are shown immediately and refetched in the background.
  3. No 404 retries: the answer cannot change by asking again; the retry function skips ApiErrors below 500.

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Item 1 B (the search text). Thinks "comes from typing into the app" makes it server-ish. Ask: who owns it, and can it change behind your back?
  • Item 2 A or C. Keys as names, not as "what the answer depends on". Lab Task 1 is the cure.
  • Item 5 B. Believes stale means "not shown". Draw the diagram again: stale = shown, then refreshed.
  • Item 6 A. Thinks retries are always kind. Ask what a fourth try at a 404 could return.
  • Item 11 A. Expects boundaries to reset queries by themselves. Show Try again with and without reset in the Network tab.
Instructor Kit · Answer Key

Session 19: Mutations and Client State

Answers and reasoning for every Session 19 item: the warm-up, both self-checks, the predict-then-check items, the lab reference outputs, the lesson pages' own quizzes and the exit ticket.

Session 19 12 self-check items Confidential

Warm-up

  1. **W1.** 7.1's homework: you pressed Publish on the planning form. What did the HQ say, and what did shoreline-api receive? **Answer:** "Ready to save", and nothing: no request. Today it sends the POST.
  2. **W2.** Write the key of the events list for Maya searching "pelican". Which short key reaches it and every other cleanup entry? **Answer:** ['events', 'list', 'q=pelican&perPage=50', 'v01'] (the search string as the page builds it); ['events'] reaches it by prefix.
  3. **W3.** Name three pieces of state in the HQ that are NOT server state (7.2's Before the Next Lesson asks for them). **Answer:** For example the search text (URL), a card's Show details (component), the confirm dialog (component), the desk's undo history (reducer), the session (session store).

7.2 · Mutations: self-check

Q1
Which of these is true of a mutation and NOT of a query?
A) It runs only when you call mutate or mutateAsync
B) Its answer is kept in the cache under a key
C) It is retried three times by default
D) It runs again when the window regains focus
Why: "Create a cleanup" must happen once, when the organizer presses Publish, not whenever a component mounts. A mutation's answer is not cached and, by default, not retried: a retried POST could create the cleanup twice.
B is wrong: Queries are cached; a mutation leaves behind a cache that is out of date, and cleaning it up is your job.
C is wrong: Queries retry by default; mutations do not (retry 0).
D is wrong: Window-focus refetching is a query's behavior.
Q2
The lesson hands useMutation(cancelEventMutation())'s parts to functions that want a number. From tsc's answer: what does mutate take, and what is data?
src/Scratch.tsx:10:17 - error TS2345: Argument of type 'number' is not assignable to parameter of type 'string'.
src/Scratch.tsx:11:15 - error TS2345: Argument of type 'CleanupEvent | undefined' is not assignable to parameter of type 'number'.
src/Scratch.tsx:12:15 - error TS2345: Argument of type 'Error | null' is not assignable to parameter of type 'number'.
Found 3 errors in the same file, starting at: src/Scratch.tsx:10
mutate takes the cleanup's id, a string; data is CleanupEvent | undefined (and error is Error | null)
Why: Every type comes from the factory: the variables from the mutation function's parameter, the data from its promise. No type arguments anywhere.
Q3
An organizer marks Diego as checked in. Which keys does the HQ invalidate afterwards?
A) ['events', 'roster', 'e17', 'v01'] only
B) ['events'] only
C) ['events'] and ['volunteers']
D) Every key in the cache
Why: "What could this change have changed?" The roster and the spots left (all under ['events']: a no-show frees a spot), and a volunteer's hours, which the Volunteer type says come from attended cleanups. Code to the contract.
A is wrong: Misses the cleanup's spots line, the lists and the volunteers' hours.
B is wrong: Misses the volunteers' hoursLogged.
D is wrong: Works, but refetches the sites and the impact totals for nothing.
Q4
Which change in the HQ is a good fit for an optimistic update?
A) Publishing a new cleanup
B) Cancelling a scheduled cleanup
C) Deleting a draft
D) A check-in mark at the desk
Why: Small, frequent, easy to undo, and the answer holds nothing you need: show it at once, roll back if the API says no. The others are big or need the API's answer (a new id).
A is wrong: The new cleanup's id comes from the API: you cannot show its page before it answers.
B is wrong: Telling 18 volunteers their Saturday is off must only happen when it is true.
C is wrong: Hard to take back; wait for the API.
Q5
The rollback in markMutation's onError was written as setStatus(signupId, saved.before). Why does tsc refuse it, and what is the fix?
src/api/mutations.ts:68:27 - error TS18048: 'saved' is possibly 'undefined'.
src/api/mutations.ts:68:27 - error TS2345: Argument of type 'SignupStatus | undefined' is not assignable to parameter of type 'SignupStatus'.
Found 2 errors in the same file, starting at: src/api/mutations.ts:68
saved is undefined when onMutate itself failed, and before is undefined when the sign-up was not in the cache; check saved?.before !== undefined first
Why: onError can run without onMutate's result, so TypeScript types the third parameter { before } | undefined. The check is not ceremony: rolling back to undefined would break the desk.
Q6
markMutation's onMutate does not call cancelQueries. The API is slow; Maya checks in Diego, then Grace a second later. What can happen to Grace's badge?
A) Nothing: optimistic updates cannot conflict
B) It flips Checked in → Expected → Checked in, when a refetch started by Diego's save lands
C) It stays Expected until the API answers
D) The second PATCH is cancelled
Why: Diego's onSettled refetched the roster; that answer was asked before Grace's change reached the API, so it overwrote her optimistic mark (measured in the lesson: two flips). cancelQueries in onMutate stops refetches already on their way.
A is wrong: Every refetch can bring an older answer back.
C is wrong: That is a waiting mutation, not an optimistic one.
D is wrong: cancelQueries cancels queries (refetches), never mutations.

7.2 · Mutations: predict, then check

P1
With the planning form's local checks turned off, an organizer publishes this. Predict the status code and how many fields get a message.
POST http://localhost:4000/api/events
{"title": "Dune", "siteId": "north-jetty", "date": "", "startTime": "",
 "endTime": "", "capacity": 500, "meetingPoint": "", ...}
HTTP/1.1 400 Bad Request
content-type: application/json; charset=utf-8

{
  "error": "validation_failed",
  "message": "Some fields need attention.",
  "fieldErrors": {
    "title": [
      "Use at least 5 characters for the title."
    ],
    "date": [
      "Enter a date as YYYY-MM-DD."
    ],
    "startTime": [
      "Enter a time as HH:MM (24-hour clock)."
    ],
    "endTime": [
      "Enter a time as HH:MM (24-hour clock)."
    ],
    "capacity": [
      "Capacity must be a whole number from 1 to 200."
    ],
    "meetingPoint": [
      "Say where volunteers meet (at least 3 characters)."
    ]
  }
}
Why: 400 and 6 messages (with the site chosen; the lesson's form, with no site, got 7). The API is the lock on the door: it checks again even when the form does, and its fieldErrors use the form's names.
P2
patchSignup's path gets ?_status=500. Predict the API's answer, the badge right after the tap, and the badge two seconds later.
PATCH http://localhost:4000/api/signups/s088?_status=500   {"status": "attended"}
HTTP/1.1 500 Internal Server Error
content-type: application/json; charset=utf-8

{
  "error": "server_error",
  "message": "Simulated 500 response (the _status parameter)."
}
Why: 500 with the API's message; the badge says Checked in at once (onMutate), then Expected again (onError rolled back), with the alert naming the person. The sign-up stayed confirmed: the kit read the roster afterwards.

7.3 · Client State: self-check

Q7
Maya wants the HQ light on her tablet, whatever the tablet says. Where does that choice live?
A) In TanStack Query's cache
B) In the URL's search params
C) In a Zustand store, persisted to localStorage
D) In sessionStorage, next to the session
Why: Not the API's business, read by components far apart, and it should outlive the tab: the last row of the lesson's table. The HQ's display store keeps it under shoreline-hq-ui.
A is wrong: There is nothing to fetch: the server never hears about it.
B is wrong: A link should not change someone's theme; it is a preference, not a place.
D is wrong: sessionStorage ends with the tab; that is right for the session token, wrong for a preference.
Q8
A teammate wrote create<UiState>(persist(…)). tsc starts like this. What is missing?
src/Scratch.ts:10:3 - error TS2345: Argument of type 'StateCreator<UiState, [], [["zustand/persist", UiState]]>' is not assignable to parameter of type 'StateCreator<UiState, [], []>'.
  Type 'StateCreator<UiState, [], [["zustand/persist", UiState]]>' is not assignable to type '{ $$storeMutators?: [] | undefined; }'.
    Types of property '$$storeMutators' are incompatible.
      Type '[["zustand/persist", UiState]] | undefined' is not assignable to type '[] | undefined'.
        Type '[["zustand/persist", UiState]]' is not assignable to type '[]'.
          Source has 1 element(s) but target allows only 0.
The empty pair of parentheses: create<UiState>()(persist(…))
Why: TypeScript cannot take some type arguments from you and infer others in one call. The first call takes UiState; the second infers persist's own types. The last line says it plainly: the middleware's types were not expected ("Source has 1 element(s) but target allows only 0").
Q9
EventCard reads the density with const { density } = useUiStore() (no selector). Fifteen cards are on screen and the person chooses Dark. How many cards re-render?
A) 15
B) 0
C) 1
D) Only the cards whose density changed
Why: Without a selector the component subscribes to the whole state, so every set re-renders it. Measured in the lesson: 15 cards on every theme change, against 0 with the selector state.density === 'compact'.
B is wrong: That is the selector version.
C is wrong: Every card subscribes on its own.
D is wrong: Nobody's density changed: all 15 re-render anyway, for nothing.
Q10
DisplaySettings reads both values with one selector that builds an object. What happens on the dev server?
const { theme, density } = useUiStore((state) => ({ theme: state.theme, density: state.density }))
A) It works, one re-render per change
B) tsc refuses the destructuring
C) Only the first value updates
D) React keeps re-rendering and gives up: Maximum update depth exceeded
Why: A new object on every call is a different answer every time (Object.is), so React never sees a stable snapshot (the console says "The result of getSnapshot should be cached"). One selector per value, or useShallow.
A is wrong: That is what useShallow gives you.
B is wrong: It type-checks and lints cleanly: only running shows it.
C is wrong: Neither value ever settles.
Q11
Someone stores {oops (not JSON at all) under shoreline-hq-ui and reloads. What does the HQ start with?
A) A crash on the first render
B) Match my device and Roomy, with no error in the console
C) The last valid choices, kept somewhere else
D) A purple theme
Why: persist catches a failed parse itself and keeps the store's defaults (measured in the lesson); merge then checks each field of anything that does parse. Data from outside is unknown until a check proves its shape.
A is wrong: persist does not throw on bad JSON.
C is wrong: There is only one saved copy.
D is wrong: There is no purple here; and merge would refuse it.
Q12
Which is a good reason to reach for Redux Toolkit instead of Zustand?
A) The codebase already uses it, or the team wants every change named and replayable
B) It has persistence built in
C) It adds fewer packages
D) It makes server data easier to cache
Why: Named actions, middleware and DevTools time travel are its strength on a large team; an existing codebase is the strongest reason of all. For the HQ's two display values Zustand was 1 package and +4.7 kB against 10 packages and +25.0 kB.
B is wrong: RTK has none: teams write it by hand or add a library such as redux-persist.
C is wrong: 10 packages against 1.
D is wrong: Server data belongs in a server-state cache (TanStack Query, or RTK Query), not in a client store.

7.3 · Client State: predict, then check

P3
A first visit to the HQ: is there a shoreline-hq-ui key in localStorage? After choosing Dark, what JSON is stored under it?
{"state":{"theme":"dark","density":"roomy"},"version":0}
Why: No key on a first visit (persist writes only after the first change), so the store's defaults are what a new person sees. After Dark, the lesson measured this JSON: the actions are left out (JSON cannot hold functions), and version 0 is persist's default.
P4
Fifteen event cards, each with useUiStore((state) => state.density === 'compact'). How many re-render when the person chooses Dark? And Compact?
Dark: 0. Compact: all 15.
Why: The lesson measured it with a Profiler around each card: the boolean selector's answer does not change on a theme change; every card's answer changes on a density change, which is right.

Lab Reference Solutions

Every output below is real: the labs run exactly as labs/check_labs.py runs them (Node 24, a terminal like a learner's, a fresh copy of the lab folder). Test durations are left out.

Lab 7.2: the volunteer's side (labs/module-19/l7_2-mutations/starter → solution)

As shipped, and the solution (npm run typecheck prints nothing after its two > lines):

Tests  7 failed (7)
Tests  7 passed (7)

The solution's factories:

export function signUpMutation() {
  return mutationOptions({
    mutationFn: (eventId: string) => postSignup(eventId),
    onSuccess: () =>
      Promise.all([
        queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['events'] }),
        queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['me'] }),
      ]),
  })
}
  • Task 1: useMutation(signUpMutation()); inside signUp.isSuccess && …, signUp.data.status is typed ('waitlisted' → the waiting-list sentence); signUp.error instanceof ApiError gives the API's 409 message, You are already signed up for this cleanup.
  • Task 2: a sign-up changes the cleanup's spots (['events', 'detail', id]) and Theo's own list (['me', 'signups']): ['events'] and ['me']. With staleTime: Infinity, nothing else would ever refresh them.
  • Task 3: the solution's cancelSignupMutation is the desk's pattern on Theo's list: onMutate awaits cancelQueries, reads the old status with getQueryData, writes 'cancelled' with setQueryData and returns { before }; onError checks saved?.before !== undefined before rolling back; onSettled invalidates ['me'] and ['events']. The tests hold the PATCH until they have seen "Cancelled", then let it fail with the API's real 500 body.
  • Stretch answer: signing up should wait: the API decides confirmed or waitlisted, so the screen cannot know the answer in advance.
Lab 7.3: pinned sites (labs/module-19/l7_3-client-state/starter → solution)

As shipped (tests, then the type check's errors), and the solution:

The solution's store and button:

togglePin: (siteId) =>
  set((state) => ({
    pinned: state.pinned.includes(siteId)
      ? state.pinned.filter((id) => id !== siteId)
      : [...state.pinned, siteId],
  })),

// in PinButton
const pinned = usePinStore((state) => state.pinned.includes(siteId))
const togglePin = usePinStore((state) => state.togglePin)
  • Task 1: a new array every time; push would change the old one (the test keeps it and checks).
  • Task 2: create<PinState>()(persist(…, { name: 'shoreline-pins', merge })); the merge returns current unless saved is an object with an array pinned, then keeps pinned.filter(isSiteId) without duplicates. The saved JSON is {"state":{"pinned":["long-strand"]},"version":0}.
  • Task 3: with const { pinned, togglePin } = usePinStore() all six buttons re-render on every pin; with the boolean selector only the clicked one.

The Lesson Pages' Own Quick Quizzes

Lesson 7.2 · Mutations
  1. D onSuccess invalidated ['events']: createEventMutation's onSuccess invalidates every key starting with events, so the list's entry is stale and is fetched again when the page shows it. Without it, the measured list still said 17.
  2. B { before: SignupStatus | undefined } | undefined: The type is inferred from what onMutate returns, plus undefined, because the mutation can fail before onMutate finished. That is why the rollback checks saved?.before.
  3. A It is a big change that is hard to take back, so the screen should say "Cancelled" only when it is true: Optimistic updates suit small, frequent, easy-to-undo changes. Cancelling affects 18 volunteers; showing it as done before it is true would mislead the organizer if the request failed.
  4. C The desk sends a second PATCH with his old status, then the reducer forgets that history entry: Undo is one more save: markMutation sends Diego's mark from before (confirmed), and when the API accepts it the reducer drops that history entry. Measured: two PATCH requests for s088.
  5. B Shows "Not saved. The server had a problem (500)…" in the form's status line and keeps what she typed: Mutations do not retry by default (a repeated POST could create the cleanup twice). mutateAsync rejects, the action's catch has no fieldErrors to show, and describeError's sentence goes in the status line; the fields keep what Maya typed.
Lesson 7.3 · Client State
  1. C In the URL's search params: A search that a link or Back should reproduce belongs in the URL's search params, where the HQ has kept it since Lesson 6.2. The query key then includes it, so the cache follows the URL.
  2. B 0: The selector still answers false, the same value as before, so Object.is says nothing changed and no card re-renders. Measured: 0, against 15 when a card read the whole store.
  3. D So you can give UiState while TypeScript still infers the middleware's types: TypeScript cannot take some type arguments from you and infer others in the same call. The first call takes UiState; the second infers the middleware's types. Without it, tsc reports TS2345 about the persist mutator.
  4. A Match my device and Compact: merge checks each saved value on its own: purple fails isTheme and falls back to the default, compact passes isDensity and is kept.
  5. B Add it to a slice the way the team does, with an action and the typed Hooks: The codebase already uses Redux Toolkit, which is the strongest reason to use it: a field and a reducer in a slice, read with the team's typed useAppSelector, keep one way of doing things.

Exit Ticket

  1. Wait and optimistic: cancel waits (big, hard to take back, 18 volunteers are told); a check-in mark is optimistic (small, frequent, easy to undo).
  2. After a mark: ['events'] (roster, spots, lists) and ['volunteers'] (hours come from attended cleanups).
  3. Compact selector: useUiStore((state) => state.density === 'compact').

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Item 1 B or C. Mixes up queries and mutations. Compare useQuery (runs on mount, cached, retried) and useMutation (runs when called) side by side.
  • Item 3 A. Invalidates only what is on screen. Ask what else a mark changes (spots, hours).
  • Item 4 B. Thinks optimistic means "faster, so better". Ask what a failure would have told 18 people.
  • Item 9 B. Believes React only re-renders what changed. Without a selector, the component subscribed to everything.
  • Item 12 B. Expects Redux Toolkit to persist by itself. It does not: Zustand's persist was the difference.

Session 20: React Hook Form + Zod and Complex Forms

Answers and reasoning for every Session 20 item: the warm-up, both self-checks, the predict-then-check items, the lab reference outputs, the lesson pages' own quizzes and the exit ticket.

Session 20 12 self-check items Confidential

Warm-up

  1. **W1.** Lesson 7.3's homework: how many places in the HQ describe a valid cleanup? Name them. **Answer:** Four: eventForm.ts's types (EventField, EventFormValues), its rules (validateEvent, toNewEvent), client.ts's isCleanupEvent (the shape coming back), and the API's own checks. The first two are today's problem.
  2. **W2.** In Lesson 3.5's form, what kept a field's message linked to its box for screen readers? **Answer:** aria-invalid on the box and aria-describedby pointing at the message's id (FieldError).
  3. **W3.** After the API answers 400 with fieldErrors, where did Lesson 7.2 put the messages? **Answer:** Under the matching fields (the API uses the form's field names), plus "Some fields need attention." in the status line.

8.1 · React Hook Form + Zod: self-check

Q1
Lesson 7.3 asked you to count the places that describe a valid cleanup. What does a Zod schema change?
A) Nothing for the types: you still write EventFormValues by hand
B) The rules and their messages are written once, and the form's types are read from the same schema
C) The API no longer needs to check anything
D) The client's response guards (isCleanupEvent) disappear
Why: One value that runs (the checks) and gives types (z.input, z.output): add a field and both follow. The API still checks everything (some rules only it can), and the HQ keeps its hand-written response guards for a measured bundle reason.
A is wrong: z.input<typeof eventFormSchema> IS the form's values type now.
C is wrong: The form is a courtesy; the API is the lock.
D is wrong: Exercise 2 shows how; the lesson keeps them, because guests would download Zod.
Q2
The lesson puts the wrong thing in each of the schema's two types. What are capacity's input and output types?
src/Scratch.ts:4:14 - error TS2322: Type 'number' is not assignable to type 'string'.
src/Scratch.ts:5:14 - error TS2322: Type 'string' is not assignable to type 'number'.
Found 2 errors in the same file, starting at: src/Scratch.ts:4
Input string (what the box holds), output number (what the checked data holds)
Why: z.coerce.number<string>() turns the text into a number before the rules run. Fields hold the input; the submit function gets the output.
Q3
What does the when option on the end-time refine do?
A) Runs the rule only on the first submit
B) Skips the rule when the times are empty
C) Runs the rule as soon as the two times have no problem, whatever the other fields say
D) Turns the rule into a warning
Why: Zod normally runs an object's refine only when every field is valid, so the end-time message would wait until the title and capacity were fixed. when says which issues matter to this rule.
A is wrong: Timing is React Hook Form's mode, not Zod's.
B is wrong: Empty times have their own field rules.
D is wrong: Zod has no warnings: a failed refine is an issue.
Q4
Where does the list of eight names in this error come from? Nobody wrote a type argument.
src/Scratch.tsx:7:19 - error TS2345: Argument of type '"titel"' is not assignable to parameter of type '"capacity" | "date" | "description" | "endTime" | "meetingPoint" | "siteId" | "startTime" | "title"'.
Found 1 error in src/Scratch.tsx:7
From the schema: zodResolver(eventFormSchema) carries its input and output types, useForm infers them, and register accepts only the input type's keys
Why: In Lesson 3.5 EventField had to be written by hand to catch the same typo; now it comes from the schema.
Q5
Why does EditEventForm check isEventField(name) before setError, instead of writing name as EventField?
A) setError is slower with a cast
B) The cast does not compile
C) Object.entries returns numbers
D) The API could send a key the form does not have, and its message would vanish into an error nobody displays
Why: The API's fieldErrors keys are plain strings (JSON from outside). A type predicate checks them for real; a cast only silences tsc.
A is wrong: Speed has nothing to do with it.
B is wrong: as EventField compiles: that is the danger.
C is wrong: Object.entries gives string keys (hence the TS2345 without the check).
Q6
Why does the HQ keep its hand-written response guards in client.ts instead of Zod schemas?
A) Zod would add about 25 kB gzip to every visitor's download, while only organizers open the forms
B) Zod cannot check API responses
C) The guards are faster at run time
D) TypeScript cannot compare a schema with types.ts
Why: Measured in the lesson: Zod's full API about 103 kB (25 kB gzip). Kept with the organizer forms, Lesson 10.1 can load it only for them. The build already went from 431.11 to 561.81 kB and warns about a chunk over 500 kB.
B is wrong: Exercise 2 does exactly that.
C is wrong: Not the reason given or measured.
D is wrong: z.ZodType<CleanupEvent> makes tsc compare them (Exercise 2).

8.1 · React Hook Form + Zod: predict, then check

P1
Weekend Cove Cleanup is full (18 of 18). An organizer saves capacity 10. The schema accepts it: what does the API answer?
PATCH http://localhost:4000/api/events/e08   {"capacity": 10}
HTTP/1.1 400 Bad Request
content-type: application/json; charset=utf-8

{
  "error": "validation_failed",
  "message": "Some fields need attention.",
  "fieldErrors": {
    "capacity": [
      "18 volunteers already hold a spot: capacity cannot be lower."
    ]
  }
}
Why: 400 with a capacity message only the API can give: it knows 18 people hold a spot. setError puts it under the capacity box and focuses it; typing a new value clears it (the schema has no objection), and the next save asks the API again.

8.2 · Complex Forms: self-check

Q7
The lesson runs this comparison with Node. Why do the impact report's counts not use z.coerce.number()?
0
Enter a whole number.
12
Coercion turns an empty box into 0, a valid count, so a skipped box would quietly report "zero"; checking the text first gives a message
Why: For capacity, 0 failed the 1-to-200 rule anyway; for a count, 0 is a real answer. Regex, then transform(Number): input string, output number.
Q8
Row 3 repeats Plastic bottles. Which path does the superRefine give its issue, so the message lands on the right box?
A) ['items']
B) ['items', 'category']
C) ['items', 2, 'category'] (the path relative to the list is [2, 'category'])
D) ['plastic-bottles']
Why: addIssue's path is relative to the array the refine sits on: [index, 'category']. The resolver turns it into items.2.category, the repeated row's menu.
A is wrong: That would be a message about the whole list, under no box.
B is wrong: No row index: which row?
D is wrong: Paths name places in the data, not values.
Q9
A keyboard user presses Remove on row 1 and the code does nothing about focus. Where is the focus afterwards?
A) On the next row's Remove button
B) On the page's body: the next Tab starts again from the top
C) On the error summary
D) Still on the removed button
Why: Measured in the lesson: document.activeElement was <body>. Whenever a click removes the element that was clicked, decide where the focus goes (the HQ: Add a row).
A is wrong: Browsers do not move focus to a sibling by themselves.
C is wrong: Nothing focuses the summary on a removal.
D is wrong: It is gone from the page.
Q10
The photo box has accept="image/jpeg,image/png,image/webp". Why does the HQ still check the file before uploading?
A) accept makes the request slower
B) The API does not check files
C) TypeScript requires it
D) accept is only a hint to the picker: "All files", drag and drop and a 40 MB photo all get past it
Why: The browser's check saves a pointless request and gives a message at once; the API checks again anyway (its answer to a GIF: 415, "Photos must be JPEG, PNG or WebP images.").
A is wrong: accept changes nothing about the request.
B is wrong: It does: 413 and 415.
C is wrong: Types say nothing about a file's content.
Q11
saveImpactMutation writes the PUT's answer with setQueryData before invalidating ['events'] and ['impact']. What does that buy?
A) The Impact tab shows the new numbers at once, instead of the old ones for a moment
B) It saves the PUT
C) It stops the dashboard from updating
D) Nothing: invalidation alone does the same
Why: Measured with --latency=1000: invalidation only showed "Volunteers: 10." then 12 a second later; with the answer written first, 12 at once. Use an answer you already have.
B is wrong: The PUT is the mutation itself.
C is wrong: ['impact'] is invalidated so the dashboard updates.
D is wrong: The measured flash says otherwise.
Q12
The error summary's links focus their field with a click handler instead of a plain href="#bags". Why?
A) Plain # links do not work in any browser
B) A # link adds #bags to the address and a history entry, so Back first undoes the jump
C) Click handlers are faster
D) TypeScript cannot type href
Why: Measured in Chromium: href="#bags" does focus the box, but writes #bags into the URL. The handler focuses and keeps the address clean.
A is wrong: Chromium did focus the box.
C is wrong: Not the point.
D is wrong: href is a string; nothing to do with it.

8.2 · Complex Forms: predict, then check

P2
A GIF sent to the photo endpoint as a raw body. Predict the status code and the message.
POST http://localhost:4000/api/photos   Content-Type: image/gif
{
  "error": "unsupported_media_type",
  "message": "Photos must be JPEG, PNG or WebP images."
}
HTTP 415
Why: 415 with a sentence written for people: the HQ shows the API's message for 413 and 415.
P3
The same endpoint, with a hand-written Content-Type: multipart/form-data header (no boundary). Predict the status and which field the API says is missing.
POST http://localhost:4000/api/photos   Content-Type: multipart/form-data
{
  "error": "validation_failed",
  "message": "Some fields need attention.",
  "fieldErrors": {
    "photo": [
      "Attach an image in a field named photo."
    ]
  }
}
HTTP 400
Why: 400: without a boundary the API cannot split the body into parts, so it never finds the photo field. Trying again would not help: leave the header to fetch.

Lab 8.1: join the crew (labs/module-20/l8_1-react-hook-form-zod/starter → solution)

Tests  8 passed (8)

The solution's schema:

name: z.string().trim()
  .min(2, { error: (issue) => (issue.input === '' ? 'Add your name.' : 'Use at least 2 characters for your name.') })
  .max(60, 'Keep your name to 60 characters or fewer.'),
email: z.email('Enter an email address like name@example.com.'),
shirtSize: z.string().pipe(z.enum(shirtSizes, 'Choose a shirt size.')),
hoursPerMonth: z.coerce.number<string>(hoursRule)
  .refine((hours) => Number.isInteger(hours) && hours >= 1 && hours <= 40, hoursRule),
  • Task 1: the one test that passes as shipped is the type test (it only fails in npm run typecheck: the starter's output type has no number). z.string().pipe(z.enum(…)) keeps the menu's '' a valid input while the output is a size.
  • Task 2: useForm({ resolver: zodResolver(joinSchema), defaultValues: emptyValues }), a fieldProps(name) helper spreading register(name) with aria-invalid and aria-describedby, handleSubmit(save), noValidate.
  • Task 3: the loop with isJoinField(name) before setError(name, …, { shouldFocus: first }), then setError('root.server', …); the unknown key volunteerId is ignored.
Lab 8.2: the supply checkout (labs/module-20/l8_2-complex-forms/starter → solution)

Tests  9 failed | 1 passed (10)

Found 1 error in src/lab.test.tsx:40

Tests  10 passed (10)

The solution's list rule and Add:

rows: z.array(rowSchema).superRefine(
  (rows, context) => { /* seen Set; addIssue({ code: 'custom', path: [index, 'item'], message }) */ },
  { when: ({ issues }) => issues.every((issue) => issue.path?.[1] !== 'item') },
),

// CheckoutForm
append({ item: next, count: '' }, { focusName: `rows.${fields.length}.count` })
  • Task 1: without when, the fourth test gets only the count message: the list rule waits until every row is valid.
  • Task 2: getValues('rows') (not fields) finds the used items; after remove(index), addRef.current?.focus(); the Add button uses aria-disabled, so it can keep the focus when all five items are listed.
  • Task 3: type first, then size with one decimal; accept would only be a hint.

The Lesson Pages' Own Quick Quizzes

Lesson 8.1 · React Hook Form + Zod
  1. B string in, number out: The type argument makes the input string (what the box holds), and coerce turns it into a number before the rules run, so the output is number. tsc showed both with TS2322.
  2. D Drop the type argument, or write useForm<EventFormValues, unknown, EventFormData>: z.infer is the output type, but the fields hold the input. Either let zodResolver infer both (no type argument) or give all three: input, context, output.
  3. A setError('capacity', …) shows the API's message under the field and moves the focus there: save catches the ApiError, checks each key with isEventField, and calls setError, which shows the message under the capacity field and focuses it. The root error fills the status line.
  4. C The title and capacity messages; the end-time message waits until they are fixed: An object's refine runs only when every field is valid, so the end-time rule waited behind the title and capacity. Measured with the test file: two messages. when lets the rule run as soon as both times are valid.
  5. B The shared schema already removed the duplicated rules, and the form needs nothing more that RHF adds: The duplicated rules were the real problem, and safeParse with the shared schema fixed that. The action form works, has no dependency, and needs none of RHF's extras such as defaultValues from saved data or dirty fields.
Lesson 8.2 · Complex Forms
  1. C field.id: useFieldArray gives every row an id that stays with its data when rows are added or removed, which is what a key is for. The index still goes in the field paths, because it says where the value is now.
  2. A So every failed Save creates a new summary, which takes the focus again, while fixing fields does not: A new key makes React create a new summary on every Save, so its effect runs again and the focus moves to it, while typing between saves only changes its list and leaves the focus alone.
  3. D The API answers 400: without the boundary it cannot find the photo field: The hand-written header has no boundary, so the API cannot split the body into parts, finds no photo field, and answers 400. With a FormData body, fetch writes the header with the boundary itself.
  4. B A union including template literal types such as `items.${number}.count`: React Hook Form builds every path from the form's input type with template literal types, so one type covers every row index and only the real field names. tsc listed them in TS2345.
  5. B A volunteer application where the questions depend on the role chosen first, ending with a review before sending: Separate tasks, later questions that depend on earlier answers, and a review before a commitment are the signs that steps help. The impact report has none of them.

Exit Ticket

  1. Capacity: string in (the box), number out (the checked data).
  2. Duplicate row: under the repeated row's menu, via addIssue with path: [index, 'category'] in the array's superRefine.
  3. No Content-Type: fetch writes multipart/form-data with the boundary; a hand-written header has none.

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Item 1 C. Thinks the schema replaces the server. Ask who can know that 18 people are signed up.
  • Item 3 B or D. Confuses Zod's when with RHF's mode. Run try-schema with and without when.
  • Item 5 B. Thinks the cast does not compile: it does, which is the danger.
  • Item 10 B. Believes the API trusts the browser. Show the 415.
  • Item 11 D. Expects invalidation to be instant. Show the measured flash.

Session 21: Testing Components

Answers and reasoning for every Session 21 item: Checkpoint 3's model answer and rubric, both self-checks, the lab's reference tests and planted bugs, the lesson page's own quiz and the exit ticket.

Session 21 12 self-check items Confidential

Checkpoint 3: Model Answer

The tests are the specification for C1 and C2; the model solution is labs/module-21/checkpoint-3/solution/. Every output below is a real run of labs/check_labs.py (a fresh copy of the folder, Node 24, a learner's terminal).

C1: src/schemas.ts
import { z } from 'zod'

const HOURS = 'Hours must be a whole number from 1 to 12.'

/** C1: the "Add a shift" form. Every value arrives as text; the output is ready for the API */
export const shiftFormSchema = z.object({
  volunteer: z.string().trim().min(2, 'Add a name (2 letters or more).'),
  date: z.string().regex(/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/, 'Pick a date.'),
  hours: z.coerce.number<string>({ error: HOURS }).int(HOURS).min(1, HOURS).max(12, HOURS),
})

/** What the form holds (text) and what the schema hands on (a number of hours) */
export type ShiftFormValues = z.input<typeof shiftFormSchema>
export type ShiftFormData = z.output<typeof shiftFormSchema>

z.coerce.number<string>(...) says the input is text, so ShiftFormValues has hours: string and ShiftFormData hours: number. The { error: HOURS } option gives the not-a-number case ('lots') the same message as the other three checks.

C2: src/queries.ts
import { mutationOptions, QueryClient, queryOptions } from '@tanstack/react-query'
import { getShifts, postShift } from './api.ts'
import type { NewShift } from './types.ts'

export const queryClient = new QueryClient({ defaultOptions: { queries: { retry: false } } })

/** C2: one site's shifts. Keys go from general to specific: every list of shifts starts with 'shifts' */
export function shiftsQuery(siteId: string) {
  return queryOptions({
    queryKey: ['shifts', siteId],
    queryFn: () => getShifts(siteId),
  })
}

/** C2: add a shift, then mark every list of shifts out of date */
export function addShiftMutation() {
  return mutationOptions({
    mutationFn: (shift: NewShift) => postShift(shift),
    onSuccess: () => queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['shifts'] }),
  })
}

Invalidating ['shifts'] (not ['shifts', siteId]) marks every site's list out of date: the test checks Driftwood Cove's list after a Pelican Point shift.

ZodError: [
  {
    "code": "custom",
    "path": [],
    "message": "TODO C1: shiftFormSchema is not written yet"

      Tests  8 failed (8)

The model solution (and npm run typecheck prints nothing after its two > lines):

 Test Files  1 passed (1)
      Tests  8 passed (8)
W1 to W4: model answers
  1. W1 Both pages read the same cache entry (the same query key), and the mutation invalidated ['events'], so every query under that key refetches (or reads the updated entry): one source of truth (Lessons 7.1 and 7.2).
  2. W2 cancelQueries for the affected key, save the previous value (getQueryData), write the new value (setQueryData); onError writes the saved value back (then invalidate on settle). Lesson 7.2's check-in mark.
  3. W3 Any two of: no refetch or invalidation (stale after a mutation elsewhere), no loading or error state, no deduplication of requests, data survives sign-out unless cleared by hand, a second source of truth that drifts from the cache. Zustand is for client state such as theme and density (Lesson 7.3).
  4. W4 Without when, Zod skips a refine until every other field is valid, so the organizer would not see the time problem until the rest was fixed. when says which fields the check needs, so it runs as soon as those two are valid.
Rubric
Criterion2 · met1 · partly0 · not yet
C1 schema: typesInput text, output number; z.input/z.output exported and usedWorks, but types are loose (any, casts)Missing or wrong
C1 schema: messagesEach field's own message, the one hours message for every bad value (0, 13, 2.5, lots)Most cases rightGeneric or no messages
C2 query factoryqueryOptions, key ['shifts', siteId] general to specific, getShiftsRight data, key order or shape offMissing
C2 mutationpostShift, then invalidates every key starting with 'shifts' (both sites)Invalidates only the current siteNo invalidation
W1 to W4All four correct in their own words, naming the mechanismTwo or three correctOne or none

9.1 · Testing Components: self-check

Q1
Which test follows "test what people see and do" best?
A) The header calls useAuth() exactly once
B) EventCard receives a site prop
C) The header's nav has the CSS Module class nav
D) An organizer's navigation has a link named Volunteers
Why: A person (and a screen reader) meets roles and names, never Hooks, props or class names. A test about the link named Volunteers survives any rewrite of the header; one about how often useAuth runs breaks on a refactor that changes nothing a person sees (Lesson 9.1, Why Tests).
A is wrong: That is how the code works inside: refactoring breaks it although nothing visible changed.
B is wrong: Props are internals; Testing Library deliberately gives you no way to read them.
C is wrong: CSS Module names are hashed in the build and say nothing about what a person gets.
Q2
You changed Jul to July in the first formatDate test on purpose. Vitest prints this. Who is wrong, the test or the app?
 FAIL  src/utils/format.test.ts > formatDate > shows a day of the crew calendar with its weekday
AssertionError: expected 'Sat, Jul 4, 2026' to be 'Sat, July 4, 2026' // Object.is equality
Expected: "Sat, July 4, 2026"
Received: "Sat, Jul 4, 2026"
A) The app: formatDate should write July
B) The test: the app writes short month names on purpose
C) Vitest: the two strings are the same
D) jsdom: it cannot format dates
Why: Read a failure like a bug report (which test, expected, received, the line), then ask the question every failure deserves. Here the expected value is the mistake: Lesson 3.4 chose short months. Changing the app's month: 'short' to 'long' instead fails two tests, and then the test is right.
A is wrong: The app's short month names are a deliberate choice from Lesson 3.4.
C is wrong: Vitest highlights exactly the letters that differ.
D is wrong: formatDate runs Intl.DateTimeFormat in Node; jsdom is not involved.
Q3
A test must say "the error summary is not on the page". Which line?
A) expect(screen.getByRole('region')).not.toBeInTheDocument()
B) expect(screen.getAllByRole('region')).toHaveLength(0)
C) expect(await screen.findByRole('region')).toBeNull()
D) expect(screen.queryByRole('region')).not.toBeInTheDocument()
Why: queryBy returns null when nothing matches, so the check can run. getBy throws before expect runs; findBy waits and then rejects; getAllBy throws on zero matches too (Lesson 9.1, the query table and Common Mistakes).
A is wrong: getByRole throws "Unable to find" first: the test fails although the summary really is gone.
B is wrong: getAllBy throws when there are no matches, like getBy.
C is wrong: findBy rejects after a second when nothing appears; it never resolves to null.
Q4
With one line commented out of the setup file, the whole suite ends like this. Which line, and why does a test that passed alone now fail?
 Test Files  3 failed | 4 passed (7)
      Tests  8 failed | 21 passed (29)
cleanup() in afterEach: every render stays on the page, so queries find the component twice ("Found multiple elements")
Why: This project imports describe/it/expect instead of using Vitest's globals, so Testing Library cannot register its own cleanup; setup.ts calls it. The tell: "Found multiple elements" in a test that worked alone (Lesson 9.1, Common Mistakes, measured: 8 of 29).
Q5
A teammate writes makeEvent({ capacity: '12' }) in a test. Vitest runs it happily. What does npx tsc -b say?
A) Nothing: tests are not type-checked
B) error TS2322: the string is not assignable to number
C) A warning, because tests may use any data
D) error TS2741: capacity is missing
Why: factories.ts takes Partial<CleanupEvent>, so every property you pass must still have the right type. Vitest strips types without checking them (like Node in Lesson 1.1); tsc -b checks the test files too, because they live in src. That is why the build still matters when the tests are green.
A is wrong: tsc -b checks every file in src, test files included.
C is wrong: TypeScript has no warnings, only errors; and test data gets no exemption.
D is wrong: Partial makes capacity optional; it is the TYPE of the value that is wrong.
Q6
getByRole('button', { name: 'Show details for Tide Pool Morning Cleanup' }) failed and listed a button named "Show detailsfor Tide Pool Morning Cleanup". The right response?
A) Switch to getByTestId so the name no longer matters
B) Expect "Show detailsfor" in the test
C) Move the space outside the visually hidden text
D) Delete the test: jsdom is wrong
Why: The test found a fragile spot: tools differ on a space at the start of hidden text. Putting it where nobody can lose it ({' '} before the hidden text) gives every tool the same name, and the test stays a check on what screen readers announce (Lesson 9.1, Reading a query's error; Lab 9.1's planted bug 4 is the same pattern).
A is wrong: That hides an accessibility question instead of answering it.
B is wrong: That pins the wrong name down forever.
D is wrong: The test was right to fail: the markup really is fragile.

Modules 7 and 8 review: self-check

Q7
In the HQ, which query key fits a volunteer's sign-ups, seen by the signed-in viewer (Lesson 7.1)?
A) ['signups', viewerId(session), volunteerId]
B) ['volunteers', volunteerId, 'signups', viewerId(session)]
C) [viewerId(session), 'volunteers']
D) ['volunteerSignups']
Why: Keys go from general to specific, with the viewer LAST on viewer-dependent data: then invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['volunteers'] }) reaches it, and signing in or out gives a separate cache entry (Lesson 7.1, queries.ts).
A is wrong: The viewer in the middle breaks the general-to-specific order.
C is wrong: Starting with the viewer means invalidating ['volunteers'] never matches it.
D is wrong: One key for every volunteer and viewer: they would overwrite each other.
Q8
A mutation succeeded. Which call makes every list of cleanups fetch again the next time it is shown?
A) queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['events'] })
B) queryClient.clear()
C) queryClient.setQueryData(['events'], [])
D) window.location.reload()
Why: invalidateQueries marks every query whose key STARTS with ['events'] as out of date: active ones refetch now, the others on their next use. Checkpoint 3's C2 asks for exactly this with ['shifts'].
B is wrong: clear() throws away every query in the cache, sessions' data included.
C is wrong: That writes an empty list as if it came from the API.
D is wrong: A reload throws away the whole app state for one list.
Q9
The check-in desk marks Diego as attended at once, before the API answers. If the PATCH fails, what must the mutation have done first (Lesson 7.2)?
A) Cancel the running roster queries and keep the old entry, so onError can put it back
B) Nothing: React Query rolls back by itself
C) Invalidate the roster before sending
D) Retry the PATCH until it succeeds
Why: An optimistic update cancels in-flight queries (so they cannot overwrite the new mark), saves the previous entry, writes the new one, and onError writes the saved one back; afterwards it invalidates. Lesson 9.2 tests exactly this rollback.
B is wrong: There is no automatic rollback: the cache only knows what you wrote.
C is wrong: Invalidating first triggers a refetch that races the optimistic write.
D is wrong: Retrying a refused change forever is not a rollback.
Q10
Which value belongs in the Zustand store rather than the TanStack Query cache (Lesson 7.3)?
A) The list of cleanups
B) The signed-in organizer's roster
C) The compact or roomy list density
D) The impact totals
Why: Client state is what only this browser knows and the API never sends: theme, density, open panels. Server state (anything the API owns) belongs in the query cache, with its loading, refetching and invalidation.
A is wrong: The API owns the cleanups: server state.
B is wrong: The roster comes from the API: server state.
D is wrong: Totals are computed by the API: server state.
Q11
In const schema = z.object({ capacity: z.coerce.number<string>() }), what are z.input<typeof schema> and z.output<typeof schema>?
A) { capacity: string } and { capacity: number }
B) { capacity: number } both
C) { capacity: unknown } and { capacity: number }
D) { capacity: string } both
Why: The form holds text (the input type) and the schema hands on a number (the output type); coerce.number<string>() says which text it accepts. React Hook Form's values use the input type, the API call the output type (Lesson 8.1).
B is wrong: The form's field values are text until the schema converts them.
C is wrong: <string> narrows the input to string; without it the input would be unknown.
D is wrong: The output is converted: that is what coerce does.
Q12
In the impact form's debris field array (Lesson 8.2), what must each row's React key be?
A) The row's index
B) The category name
C) The id that useFieldArray gives each row
D) A random number made while rendering
Why: useFieldArray gives every row a stable id; removing row 2 then keeps row 3's input (and its value and focus) instead of shifting everything up. Indexes move when rows are removed; categories can be empty or repeated while typing; random keys remount every render.
A is wrong: Indexes shift on remove, so React reuses the wrong inputs.
B is wrong: A category can be empty or chosen twice while the organizer types.
D is wrong: A new key on every render throws every input away.

Every output is real: the lab runs exactly as labs/check_labs.py runs it (Node 24, a learner's terminal, a fresh copy of the lab folder). Vitest's per-test times are left out.

Lab 9.1: labs/module-21/l9_1-testing-components/starter → solution

Task 1, src/test/factories.ts and src/hours.test.ts:

// Sample data for tests: complete, correctly typed objects. A test passes only the parts it is about.
import type { Shift } from '../types.ts'

/** A worked 3-hour shift at Pelican Point Beach */
export function makeShift(changes: Partial<Shift> = {}): Shift {
  return {
    id: 'sh1',
    volunteer: 'v04',
    site: 'Pelican Point Beach',
    date: '2026-07-04',
    hours: 3,
    status: 'worked',
    ...changes,
  }
}
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'
import { badgeFor, formatHours, hoursWorked } from './hours.ts'
import { makeShift } from './test/factories.ts'

describe('hoursWorked', () => {
  it('adds up the hours of the worked shifts', () => {
    const shifts = [makeShift({ hours: 3 }), makeShift({ id: 'sh2', hours: 2.5 })]
    expect(hoursWorked(shifts)).toBe(5.5)
  })

  it.each(['planned', 'missed'] as const)('counts a %s shift as 0 hours', (status) => {
    expect(hoursWorked([makeShift({ status })])).toBe(0)
  })

  it('is 0 with no shifts at all', () => {
    expect(hoursWorked([])).toBe(0)
  })
})

describe('badgeFor', () => {
  it.each([
    [0, 'none'],
    [9.5, 'none'],
    [10, 'bronze'],
    [24.5, 'bronze'],
    [25, 'silver'],
    [50, 'gold'],
  ] as const)('gives %s hours the badge %s', (hours, badge) => {
    expect(badgeFor(hours)).toBe(badge)
  })
})

describe('formatHours', () => {
  it('writes one hour in the singular', () => {
    expect(formatHours(1)).toBe('1 hour')
  })

  it('writes every other amount in the plural', () => {
    expect(formatHours(2.5)).toBe('2.5 hours')
  })
})
